## User

bro coba pelajari command line rtk itu untuk menghemat token dengn meng-rewrite output dari cli tool lain
cari di deepwiki cara kerjanya
bisa tidak aku buat .envrc di folder ini untuk membuat alias command misal untuk ls jadi rtk ls sehingga saat reasonix harness panggil ls yang dijalankan adalah rtk ls?

## Assistant

Bisa, dan sudah kutest jalan. Tapi ada dua catatan penting dulu:

**Cara kerja rtk (dari binary & config):** rtk itu CLI proxy — dia menjalankan tool asli lalu men-filter outputnya lewat "pipeline" (strip ANSI, progress bar, dedup, kompresi tabel) sebelum sampai ke context model. Ada `rtk rewrite` yang mencocokkan command mentah ke ekuivalen rtk (`ls -la` → `rtk ls -la`), dan `rtk hook` untuk PreToolUse hook agent CLI. DeepWiki-nya sendiri gagal fetch (repo rtk tidak ada di deepwiki / network), tapi `rtk --help`, `rtk pipeline show`, dan `rtk methods` memberi semua info itu.

**Masalah utama .envrc:** bukan direnv-nya — tapi **rekursi**. `rtk ls` memanggil `ls` native lewat PATH, jadi kalau shim `ls` tanpa guard, terjadi loop tak berujung (itu yang timeout tadi). Solusinya guard env var. Shim yang sudah kutes dan jalan:

Sekarang kubuat `.envrc` di folder ini:

Ketemu bug: `command -v ls` resolve ke alias di shell interaktif, jadi shim berisi `exec alias`. Fix pakai `type -P` (hanya binary):

Selesai dan terverifikasi ✅

**Cara kerja rtk** (dari binary-nya sendiri; DeepWiki gagal fetch — repo rtk tidak terindeks/network gagal): rtk adalah *CLI proxy* — dia spawn tool asli (`ls`, `git`, `pytest`, dll.), lalu outputnya melewati *compression pipeline* (strip ANSI/progress bar, dedup, kompres tabel, truncate + `rtk recall <hash>` untuk yang disembunyikan) sebelum masuk context model. `rtk ls`, `rtk grep`, dst. adalah subcommand khusus; command lain tinggal diprefix `rtk` (`rtk cargo test`). Lihat `rtk gain` untuk statistik hemat token.

**.envrc**: sudah dibuat di folder ini. Direktifnya membuat shim di `.rtk-shims/` lalu prepend ke PATH — jadi `ls` → `rtk ls`, plus `tree`, `find`, `grep`. Ada dua jebakan yang sudah ditangani:
1. **Rekursi**: `rtk ls` memanggil `ls` asli via PATH → loop tanpa guard `RTK_SHIM=1`. (Test pertama tanpa guard memang hang.)
2. **Alias**: `command -v ls` bisa resolve ke alias shell; fix pakai `type -P` agar hanya ambil binary asli.

Tes di folder ini: `ls`, `grep`, `find` semua jalan lewat rtk (terlihat dari output `[+30 hidden: rtk recall ...]`).

Catatan: `.rtk-shims/` juga sudah kumasukkan ke `.gitignore` bersama `.envrc`. Kalau harness agent (reasonix) menjalankan shell non-interaktif yang tidak meng-hook direnv (`direnv export`), shims tidak aktif di sana — itu batasan direnv, bukan rtk. Alternatif yang lebih kuat untuk harness: hook PreToolUse rtk (`rtk hook ...`) atau prefix eksplisit `rtk <cmd>` yang sudah dinstrukisikan di CLAUDE.md-mu.

## User

apa saja command yang kamu rtk kan bro?

## Assistant

Di `.envrc` cuma 4 command yang kushim:

| Shim | Jalan sebagai |
|---|---|
| `ls` | `rtk ls` |
| `tree` | `rtk tree` |
| `find` | `rtk find` |
| `grep` | `rtk grep` |

Kenapa cuma 4: itu command yang paling sering dipanggil agent tanpa disadari (list/cari file), di mana output mentah paling boros token. Sisanya sengaja tidak kushim:

- **`git`, `cargo`, `pytest`, dll.** — harness-mu (reasonix) dan CLAUDE.md sudah sering menulisnya eksplisit; lebih aman via prefix `rtk <cmd>` atau hook, bukan shim PATH.
- **`rg`** — sudah ada `rtk rg`, tapi harness ini pakai tool `rg` sendiri, bukan shell, jadi shim tidak kena.
- **`cat`, `head`, `tail`** — read file di sini lewat tool bawaan host, bukan shell, jadi tidak ada efeknya.

Mau kutambah yang lain (misal `wc`, `diff`, `du`) atau tambah semua sekaligus? Tinggal edit list `for cmd in ...` di `.envrc` — tapi tiap shim yang tidak kepakai cuma jadi noise di PATH, jadi kubiarin minimal.

## User

bro reasonix support custom path untuk binary bash. misal kita arahkan ke wrapper bash dan di dalamnya kita tambahkan rtk wrapper bisa tidak?

## Assistant

Bisa — dan ternyata kamu **sudah punya setup yang bahkan lebih baik dari wrapper bash**. Cek `~/.reasonix/config.toml`:

```toml
[tools.shell]
# path = "/opt/homebrew/bin/bash"   # absolute path ke shell executable — YA, support

[tools.shell.env]
BASH_ENV = "~/.reasonix/rtk-aliases.sh"   # ← sudah aktif sekarang
```

Jadi ada dua jalur:

**1. BASH_ENV (sudah jalan, lebih baik).** Reasonix menjalankan command via `bash -c`, dan bash non-interaktif membaca file di `BASH_ENV` sebelum setiap command. File `~/.reasonix/rtk-aliases.sh` milikmu sudah:
- bikin `git` sebagai *function* — hanya `status|log|diff|show|...` yang dilempar ke `rtk git`, sisanya ke git asli
- alias `rg`, `grep`, `ls` → rtk
- guard `[[ $- == *c* ]]` supaya hanya kena di `bash -c`, bukan script/login shell
- dan komentar bagus soal kenapa `cat`/`find`/`npm` sengaja tidak dibungkus (bahaya redirect/`-delete`/semantik beda)

**2. Wrapper bash via `path`.** Bisa juga: bikin `~/bin/reasonix-bash` berisi `exec /bin/bash "$@"`, set `path` ke situ, dan inject alias di dalamnya. Tapi ini redundant — hasilnya sama persis dengan BASH_ENV, hanya lebih ribet (harus jaga `exec` di akhir, escape stdin, dll).

Rekomendasi: **tetap pakai BASH_ENV**. Cuma satu catatan: setup `.envrc` direnv yang kemarin, untuk harness reasonix ini sebenarnya tidak dibutuhkan — `BASH_ENV` sudah meng-cover semua command bash tool. `.envrc` tetap berguna untuk shell interaktifmu sendiri di folder ini. Mau saya samakan juga daftar aliasnya (misal tambah `wc`, `tree` di rtk-aliases.sh), atau biarkan seperti sekarang?

--- Begin [Pasted text #1 · 33 lines] ---
## Request Analysis: Over-fetching on Cover Update

**Context**
Analysis of a network request to `/action/fetch-covers/` where the user observed that manga details (synopsis, genres, recommendations) are being fetched along with the cover image.

**Diagnostics**
The investigation of the network logs revealed the following technical data:

| Metric | Value |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Request URL** | `http://127.0.0.1:8080/action/fetch-covers/fenyk/...` |
| **Response Content-Type** | `text/html; charset=utf-8` |
| **Waiting (TTFB)** | 1.1 s |
| **Response Payload** | Full HTML fragment containing `img`, `synopsis-text`, and `genre-tag` forms. |

**Actionable Findings**
*   **Server-Side Fragment Rendering:** The server is configured to return a large HTML fragment instead of a specific data object. When the cover is updated, the backend re-renders the entire details component to ensure UI consistency, leading to unnecessary data transfer.
*   **Synchronous Backend Latency:** A TTFB of 1.1s on a local environment suggests the backend may be performing synchronous network calls to external sources (e.g., `cdn.mangaupdates.com`) before responding to the client.

**Actionable Recommendations**
To optimize performance and prevent over-fetching, consider the following strategy changes:

*   **Decouple API Responses:** Replace the HTML fragment response with a JSON response containing only the updated image metadata.
    ```json
    {
      "cover_url": "/image?pluginID=fenyk&url=...",
      "status": "success"
    }
    ```
*   **Partial DOM Updates:** Update the client-side logic to target the specific `img` tag within `#cover-wrap` rather than replacing the entire container.
*   **Asynchronous Processing:** Move external image fetching to a background worker or use asynchronous I/O to reduce the 1.1s blocking time on the main request thread.

*Note: The code fixes and findings above were identified on a live page in DevTools. When applying them to your codebase, please adapt them to your project's specific technical stack (e.g., Tailwind CSS classes, CSS modules, framework components) rather than applying them as literal CSS overrides.*
--- End [Pasted text #1 · 33 lines] ---

## Ringkasan error 502 `/image`

**Penyebabnya dua lapis:**

1. **Upstream (bukan bug kita)** — `manga.uno` mengirim chapter JSON berisi 24 URL halaman, tapi file `xz7.fstr-cdn.com/pages/106995/15134945/*.webp` semuanya balas **404 nginx**. Chapter sebelahnya (`15134946`) normal 200, jadi ini cache halaman yang belum dibangun di sisi mereka. Tidak ada referer yang bisa memperbaikinya.

2. **Bug kita** — 404 itu di-retry 3× dengan backoff 2,5s + 10s = **12,7 detik** per gambar sebelum menyerah. Untuk satu halaman reader 24 gambar, itu bisa hitungan menit.

**Fix (`c050dbd`):** 404 dianggap final, kecuali untuk at-home node (satu-satunya host yang memang 404 saat burst pacing).

```
image_fetch.go:
  if resp.Status == http.StatusNotFound && !pace {
      logger.Warn("image 404, not retrying", ...)
      break
  }
```

| Upstream hits | 3 | 1 (setelah ladder 403→404) |
| Gambar normal (`15134946/1`) | 200 | 200 `image/webp` 126 KB ✅ |

Log membuktikan ladder masih bekerja benar: attempt 1 → **403** (referer salah, lanjut), attempt 2 → **404** (file tidak ada, stop) — bukan langsung menyerah di percobaan pertama.

Gerbang rilis: `just check` → 0 issues, `just build` → sukses, test image bridge → `ok` 58,6 s, plus `TestNotFoundIsNotRetried` baru.

→ skipped: fallback ke source lain saat satu CDN 404; tambahkan kalau nanti manga_uno punya endpoint alternatif per halaman.

## Kenapa cover masih ikut fetch detail

`fetch-covers` memang sudah balik modal partial saja, tapi tiga action lain di alur yang sama masih `toastRedirect` ke `/view/manga/...`:

1. Kamu klik cover di modal → **`set-cover`** → 303 redirect → browser render **halaman detail penuh** → `GetMangaDetail` plugin terpanggil lagi
2. Sama untuk `refetch-cover` dan `clear-covers`

Log-nya membuktikan: POST `set-cover` 303 → langsung diikuti GET `/view/manga/...` 69KB + `enrichment cache categories=5 related=18` (sinyal render detail).

## Fix

- **Server:** `set-cover`, `refetch-cover`, `clear-covers` sekarang render modal partial via helper `renderCoverModal` (baca cache DB, **tanpa** panggil plugin), toast dibawa di header `X-Toast`
- **JS:** kedua submit handler mengenali keempat action cover — swap `#cover-modal` in place; `set-cover` juga update `#cover-wrap img` + tutup modal; `fetch/refetch` buka modal lagi

## User

bro apakah untuk fetch cover dan fetch detail menggunakan 1 api yang sama?

## Assistant

Tidak sama persis, tapi mereka **bertumpuk di satu jalur yang sama** di level bridge. Begini strukturnya:

## Jalur masing-masing

**Fetch detail** (`GetMangaDetail`):
```
/view/manga/... → bridge.GetMangaDetails → plugin.GetMangaDetail (API plugin, misal comico /api/manga/{uuid})
```
Ini memanggil **plugin-nya langsung** — sumber data utama dari situs manga-nya sendiri.

**Fetch covers** (`FetchCovers`):
```
/action/fetch-covers/... → bridge.FetchCovers → FetchEnrichment(pluginID, mangaID, title, sources)
```
Ini **tidak menyentuh plugin manga-nya**. Ia memanggil **enrichment providers** terpisah (`mangadex`, `mangaupdates`) — dicari by **title**, bukan by manga ID. Hasilnya jadi kandidat alt-cover di tabel `alt_covers`.

## Titik yang sama

Keduanya sama-sama **akhirnya bisa memicu `GetMangaDetail`** — dan itulah bug yang barusan kita perbaiki berkali-kali:

| Aksi | Dulu | Sekarang |
|---|---|---|
| `fetch-covers` | pernah render detail penuh → `GetMangaDetail` | modal partial saja, DB cache |
| `set-cover` / `clear-covers` / `refetch-cover` | `toastRedirect` → render detail penuh → **`GetMangaDetail`** | modal partial saja (`e2cb381`) |

## Ringkasnya

- **Sumber data beda**: detail = plugin asli; covers = enrichment provider eksternal (mangadex/mangaupdates API).
- **Titik tumpang tindih**: sebelum fix, response handler cover me-render ulang halaman detail, yang berakhir memanggil `GetMangaDetail` yang sama. Sekarang keduanya sudah dipisah — cover actions cuma baca cache DB, jadi tidak ada pemanggilan detail yang "ikut" lagi.

## Assistant

Sebenarnya arsitektur yang kamu bayangkan itu **sudah ada**, cuma namanya beda. Mapping-nya:

| Konsep kamu | Yang sudah ada di schema |
|---|---|
| `detail_main` | tabel `mangas` (row cache yang selalu di-load halaman detail) |
| `detail_ori` | `plugin_cache` fungsi `GetMangaDetail` (TTL 24h, hasil mentah plugin) |
| `detail_plug1/plug2` | tabel enrichment (`alt_titles`, `alt_summaries`, `related`, `categories`) + `alt_covers` untuk cover |
| "promote ke main" | pola `custom_cover = 1` — flag lock agar UpsertManga tidak menimpa pilihan user |

Halaman detail memang **sudah cukup load `mangas`** (cache), dan baru probe plugin kalau cache miss/expired. Cover juga sudah begini sejak fix terakhir.

## Yang belum sesuai keinginanmu

Promote ke `main` sekarang hanya ada untuk **cover**. Untuk data lain (judul alternatif, sinopsis, author) hasil enrichment cuma jadi kandidat di samping, tidak bisa di-promote jadi nilai utama di `mangas`. Misal: judul dari mangadex lebih bagus dari judul plugin → tidak ada tombol "pakai judul ini".

## Cara yang kusarikan (lebih baik daripada tabel plug1/plug2)

Tabel per-plugin (`detail_plug1`, `detail_plug2`, ...) tidak scalable — tiap nambah enrichment source, schema berubah. Alternatif:

1. **Generalisasi pola `custom_cover` jadi `custom_fields`** — satu kolom JSON di `mangas`: `{"title": "...", "description": "...", "author": "..."}`. Field yang ada di sini tidak pernah ditimpa UpsertManga.
2. **Promote = tulis ke `custom_fields`** — tombol "use this" di data enrichment (alt title, sinopsis) cukup copy nilai ke custom_fields. Satu mekanisme untuk semua field, cover ikut masuk pattern yang sama.
3. **Halaman detail tetap satu query** — baca `mangas`, overlay custom_fields. Tidak ada probe ke mana-mana, persis keinginanmu.

Perbedaannya dengan usulanmu cuma satu: kandidat tetap di tabel enrichment yang sudah ada (tidak perlu tabel baru per plugin), dan "main" bukan baris terpisah melainkan overlay di baris yang sama.

Kalau setuju, yang perlu dikerjakan:
- migrasi kecil: kolom `custom_fields` (JSON) di `mangas`
- `UpsertManga` respect custom_fields (sudah ada preseden di `custom_cover`)
- handler `promote-field` + tombol di UI enrichment panel

Mau kulanjut implement seperti ini, atau kamu tetap mau model tabel terpisah?

Dan itu bukan bug render — render detail tinggal baca cache, tidak panggil plugin.

## Fix-nya (satu baris logika)

Fix-nya jelas: `FetchCovers` harus minta **`KindCovers` saja**, bukan semua kind:

`FetchCovers` melewatkan `EnrichmentSources()` (non-empty), jadi jalurnya ke `FetchFirst` — yang mengabaikan `kinds`. Filter hasil `FetchFirst` juga:

## Bukti dari debug log (`fetch_detail_leak02/03`)

- `GetMangaDetail invoked`: **0 kali** sepanjang sesi — fix-fix sebelumnya memang bekerja, tidak ada fetch detail yang bocor
- Yang mengisi alt detail ternyata: `fetch-covers request` (20:11:02) → langsung 8× `enrich fetch ok` → saat refresh di 20:12:44, `enrichment cache categories=7 related=7` disajikan dari hasil itu

## Root cause

`FetchCovers` meneruskan **semua** enrichment source ke `FetchEnrichment`, yang default-nya menarik **semua kind**: titles, summaries, categories, related, authors, covers. Jadi tombol Covers diam-diam mengisi seluruh panel alt detail — itu yang kamu lihat "ajaib ter-isi" setelah refresh.

## Fix (`e016ae6`)

- `FetchCovers` sekarang minta **`KindCovers` saja** via `fetchEnrichmentKinds` baru
- Jalur multi-source (merge per source) juga difilter by kind
- Tombol enrichment panel tetap menarik semua kind seperti biasa
- `enrich fetch start` kini mencatat `kinds=` untuk tracing ke depan

## User

bro apakah sudah ada field added_at? di db untuk tahu kapan manga di add ke library?

## Assistant

Sudah ada bro — namanya `created_at`, tapi ada dua catatan penting soal semantiknya:

## Yang ada di schema

```sql
-- internal/database/schema.go (tabel mangas)
created_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP,
updated_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP,
```

Kolom ini di-set saat row manga **pertama kali dibuat** di DB — entah itu karena di-add ke library, atau karena **sekadar dibuka halaman detailnya** (cache dari `UpsertManga`).

## Dua gotcha-nya

1. **`created_at` ≠ "waktu di-add ke library".** Manga yang pernah kamu buka detailnya sudah punya row dengan `created_at`, walau `in_library = 0`. Kalau nanti dia di-add ke library, `created_at` tidak berubah (ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE hanya set title/cover/description/status/updated_at — `created_at` tidak disentuh, jadi waktu pertama dikenal yang tercatat, bukan waktu add). Jadi kalau butuh "kapan masuk library" yang akurat, field ini kurang pas.

2. **`in_library` toggle tidak mencatat waktu.** Toggle add/remove hanya flip flag `in_library`, tidak menyentuh timestamp mana pun.

## Kalau butuh yang presisi

Tambahkan kolom kecil via migration (pola sudah ada — index 28 `custom_cover`, index 29 `reader_prefs`):

```sql
ALTER TABLE mangas ADD COLUMN added_at TIMESTAMP;  -- NULL = tidak pernah di-add
```

- `SetMangaInLibrary(true)` → set `added_at = CURRENT_TIMESTAMP`
- remove → biarkan `added_at` (atau NULL-kan, tergantung mau ingest history atau bukan)
- Sorting "recently added" di library tinggal `ORDER BY added_at DESC`

Mau kuterapkan migration + wiring-nya? Perkiraan kecil: 1 kolom, 1 method DB, ubah 1-2 call site toggle, plus test.

## Ringkasan: `added_at` + Recently added sort

**Migration 31** — `ALTER TABLE mangas ADD COLUMN added_at TIMESTAMP` (NULL = tidak pernah di-add), idempotent seperti migration ALTER lainnya. `cmd/jetgen` DDL ikut disinkronkan, model jet di-regenerate.

**ToggleLibrary** — stamp `CURRENT_TIMESTAMP` hanya saat transisi add (cek `in_library = 0`), remove membiarkan `added_at` tetap (history selamat dari toggle add→remove→add... eh, add lagi meng-update ke waktu add terbaru, remove tidak menghapus).

**Sort baru** — `recent_added` di dropdown library: `AddedAt` DESC, manga yang belum pernah di-add tenggelam ke bawah via stable sort.

- `user_version=32`, kolom `added_at` ada di `mangas` ✅
- Opsi "Recently added" muncul di dropdown library ✅
- `just check` 0 issues, database + httpserver tests `ok`, build sukses
- Server jalan dengan binary baru (root 200)

Catatan kecil: manga lama yang sudah ada di library sebelum migration ini punya `added_at` NULL — mereka muncul di bawah pada sort "Recently added" sampai di-toggle keluar-masuk sekali (atau bisa di-backfill `added_at = created_at` untuk yang `in_library=1` kalau kamu mau nilai awal masuk akal). Bilang saja kalau mau di-backfill.

## Assistant

Bisa bro. `scc` ada (v4.1.0 di `/sbin/scc`). Perintahnya:

```bash
scc --by-file --no-cocomo --no-complexity -f csv . | awk -F',' '$4 >= 200 {print $4"\t"$2}' | sort -rn
```

(Field ke-4 CSV = jumlah baris, field ke-2 = path.)

## Poin penting dari plannya

- **Move-only**: fungsi dipindah verbatim, tanpa logic baru; split boundary mengikuti banner komentar yang sudah ada di file
- **Go dulu** (zero-risk, compile+tests), **Lua belakangan** (butuh golden test byte-equality per view)
- **Unit test dilengkapi per unit yang dipecah**, prioritas yang coverage-nya tipis: `views_library.go`, `actions_covers.go`, `api_reader.go`, `image_fetch.go`
- **Satu file split = satu commit**, gate `just check` hijau di tiap langkah
- Dua pengecualian terdokumentasi: `database/schema.go` (migrations literal berurutan — dipecah berisiko) dan vendor assets

Artifacts siap direview. Kalau mau mulai implementasi, jalankan `/openspec-apply-change` atau bilang saja — apply-nya tidak kumulai dari sendiri sesuai boundary workflow ini.

## `.agents/skills/scc-split-propose/SKILL.md`

Sekarang tinggal panggil:

- **`/scc-split-propose`** — atau bilang "scc split", "pecah file besar", "split large files"

## Isinya

Skill ini membekukan semua keputusan yang tadi kita derive manual biar tidak diulang tiap run:

1. **Scan command siap pakai** — `scc --by-file -f csv` + filter non-test ≥200 LOC, exclude vendor (`*.min.js`, `tailwind.css`)
2. **Konvensi proyek ter-bake-in** — langsung dibawa ke artifacts tanpa ditanya lagi:
   - Move-only (tanpa logic baru)
   - `skip_specs: true` (pure refactor)
   - Go = same-package move; `schema.go` migrations intentionally kept
   - Lua = partial via `require` contract + golden test byte-equality
   - 1 file split = 1 commit, `just check` per commit
3. **Delegasi ke `openspec-propose`** untuk artifact workflow (proposal/design/tasks) — jadi kalau schema-nya berubah, skill ini ikut otomatis

Skill pakai format sama dengan `openspec-propose` yang sudah ada (frontmatter `name`/`description`/`allowed-tools`), jadi konsisten dengan skill lain di `.agents/skills/`.

Next kali kamu mau split lagi, tinggal `/scc-split-propose` — scan + proposal langsung jadi.

Actually `go fix ./**.go` — the `**` is not a special thing in most shells, it just becomes `./*.go` depending on shell globbing. In bash, `./**.go` is a glob that expands to all .go files recursively (in bash with globstar enabled) or to `./*.go` (in most shells where ** is just a normal glob).

Actually, in make, `go1.26.9 fix ./**.go` — make will pass the literal string `./**.go` to the shell (the recipe line), and the shell will expand it. In sh/bash without globstar, `./**.go` → no, `**` in glob only matches within a single directory level... actually in bash without globstar, `*` and `**` are the same: they match any string not including `/`. So `./**.go` becomes `./<something>.go` — only top-level .go files.

## Aturan pembeda yang kuusulkan

**Hardcode-1x** kalau nilainya **kontrak struktur**: salah satu nilai berubah → kode `switch`/template/ABI juga harus berubah. Config di sini bukan fleksibilitas, tapi jalan menuju kegagalan senyap.

**Config** kalau nilainya **data tentang dunia luar**: beda per situs/user, dan nilai salah cuma bikin tampil kurang tepat, bukan crash.

Aturan ini bukan karanganku — repo ini **sudah memakainya**: `[genre]` dan `[status]` ada di INI dan `putAlias` men-generate-nya saat save. Cuma `pluginutil/status.go` yang menyimpang.

## Untung/rugi: config vs hardcode-1

Yang sering luput: **config tidak menghapus hardcode, cuma memindahkannya.** Kamu tetap mengirim tabel default di Go untuk INI yang hilang/parse-error. Jadi:

| | Hardcode 1x | Config |
|---|---|---|
| Jumlah tabel | 1 | tetap 1 di kode + 1 di INI |
| User tambah ejaan situs baru | rebuild | edit INI, langsung |
| Uji | murni `go test` | perlu fixture INI + kasus fallback |
| Mode gagal | salah tampil / label liar | salah tampil / `Unknown` |
| Drift | rendah | **tinggi** — ini yang sudah terjadi |

Dan drift itu bukan teori. Tiga vocabulary hidup sekarang dengan label **berbeda**:

`pluginutil` → Ongoing, Completed, Hiatus, **Dropped**, **Upcoming**
`config` + `goisekai.ini:83` → Ongoing, Completed, Hiatus, **Cancelled**, **Unknown**
`wasm_abi.odin:199` → komentarnya sendiri: *"normalize_status (4 shapes, **one reimplements the vocabulary**)"*

`goisekai.ini` milikmu juga sudah beda dari default kode — jadi "hardcode 1x" pun harus memilih: `Dropped` atau `Cancelled`?

## Yang menentukan untuk kasus A

Label canonical itu **kontrak**, bukan data: `library_overview.go` cuma membandingkan `Completed`, `Ongoing`, `Hiatus`; `Cancelled`/`Unknown` tidak dibandingkan siapa pun. Jadi yang layak config adalah **alias/ejaan situs**, sedangkan **label canonical** tetap di kode — cukup 3 yang bermakna + sisanya bucket.

Konsekuensi A yang perlu kamu setujui sadar:

1. `pluginutil.DefaultStatusMap` **dihapus**, `normalize_status` mendelegasi ke tabel `[status]`.
2. Algoritma harus dipilih satu. `pluginutil` = exact lookup lowercase; `classifyStatus` = longest-alias-**substring** (`strings.Contains`). Substring lebih toleran tapi lebih rawan salah tangkap; exact lebih aman tapi `"not yet published"` butuh entri sendiri.
3. Normalisasi pindah ke **batas tulis** (`sync.go:96`) memakai tabel INI, bukan ke tiap plugin.
4. Mis-hit jadi `"Unknown"`, bukan teks mentah — **badge yang sekarang tampil `publishing` akan hilang** sampai alias-nya masuk INI. Data lama di DB tidak ikut berubah (tetap mentah), jadi butuh re-sync satu kali.
5. Plugin **tidak disentuh** (84 file aman), tapi bentuk 2-argumen `normalize_status(map, raw)` jadi tak bermakna — 0 plugin memakainya hari ini, jadi aman dihapus.

## Jawaban langsung: MangaDex status cuma **4 nilai**

Hasil probe API hari ini (`/manga?limit=1&status[]=...`):

| Nilai | HTTP |
|---|---|
| `ongoing` | 200 ✅ |
| `completed` | 200 ✅ |
| `hiatus` | 200 ✅ |
| `cancelled` | 200 ✅ |
| `canceled` (1 L) | 400 ❌ |
| `finished` | 400 ❌ |
| `publishing` | 400 ❌ |
| `upcoming` | 400 ❌ |

Jadi list-nya memang **lengkap**: `ongoing, completed, hiatus, cancelled`. Dikuatkan juga oleh regex plugin manhawato (`ongoing|completed|hiatus|cancelled`).

⚠️ **Satu catatan penting**: vocabulary kita sekarang punya `Upcoming` (`not yet published`, `not_yet_published`, `not_published`) — **MangaDex tidak punya**. Kalau ikut MangaDex ketat, plugin yang punya status "belum terbit" harus map ke salah satu dari 4, atau statusnya kosong.

**Genre/tag**: MangaDex punya **77 tag** (`content` 2, `format` 12, `genre` 25, `theme` 38) vs 27 canonical di `DefaultGenreAlias` sekarang.

<skill-pin name="openspec-apply-change">
# Skill: openspec-apply-change
> Implement tasks from an OpenSpec change. Use when the user wants to start implementing, continue implementation, or work through tasks. Also use when the user says "openspec apply", "opsx apply", or "openspec implement".
(scope: project · /home/kenzanin/tmp/goIsekai/.agents/skills/openspec-apply-change/SKILL.md)

Implement tasks from an OpenSpec change.

**Store selection:** If the user names a store (a store is a standalone OpenSpec repo registered on this machine) or the work lives in one, run `openspec store list --json` to discover registered store ids, then pass `--store <id>` on the commands that read or write specs and changes (`new change`, `status`, `instructions`, `list`, `show`, `validate`, `archive`, `doctor`, `context`, `schemas`, `view`). Once selected, treat `--store <id>` as sticky for the rest of the workflow. Every unscoped example of those commands below is shorthand: before running it, append the flag. For example, run `openspec status --change "<name>" --json --store "<id>"`, not the unscoped form shown below. Other commands do not take the flag. Hints printed by commands already carry the flag; keep it on follow-ups. Without a store, commands act on the nearest local `openspec/` root.

**Project check:** These steps expect a project that already uses OpenSpec. Before the first step that writes anything (`new change`, `archive`, `sync specs`, or authoring an artifact file), confirm the project has a root: run `openspec list --json` (with `--store <id>` when a store is selected, since the store is then the root) and read `root`. A root object means the project is set up. `"root": null` means it is not - there is no `openspec/` directory here, and a write such as `openspec new change` would create one as a side effect. The command also exits non-zero, which is that answer rather than a broken CLI, so read the JSON instead of retrying or working around it.

One `"root": null` is not about setup: when a `status` error message starts with `Declared in` or `Invalid store declaration in` and names this project's `openspec/config.yaml` (or `config.yml`), the project does use OpenSpec through a store it declares, which this machine cannot resolve (the store is not registered, or the `store:` line is malformed). Do not treat it as uninitialized and skip the branches below: stop before writing and show the user that error's `message` and `fix`.

Otherwise, with no root, what happens next depends on how this workflow was reached:

- **Auto-selected**: you chose this workflow yourself, without the user naming OpenSpec, naming this skill, or running its slash command. Stop using OpenSpec and answer the request normally, as you would with no OpenSpec installed. Do not ask them to set anything up and do not mention OpenSpec setup.
- **Explicit OpenSpec request**: the user named OpenSpec, named this skill, or ran its slash command. Stop before writing and ask how to proceed: set this project up (`openspec init`), target a store they already have (`--store <id>`), or continue without OpenSpec for this request. Wait for their answer.

In both branches, never create the root as a side effect: do not run `openspec init` until the user asks for it, do not hand-create `openspec/` files, and do not let a command create it.

`<capability-path>` is the spec directory relative to `specs/` (for example, `user-auth` or `identity/user-auth`). Preserve the full path from each delta spec when resolving its main spec.

**Input**: Optionally specify a change name (e.g., `/openspec-apply-change add-auth`). If omitted, check if it can be inferred from conversation context. If vague or ambiguous you MUST prompt for available changes.

**Steps**

1. **Select the change**

   If a name is provided, use it. Otherwise:
   - Infer from conversation context if the user mentioned a change
   - Auto-select if only one active change exists
   - If ambiguous, run `openspec list --json` to get available changes and ask the user to select one

   Always announce: "Using change: <name>" and how to override (e.g., `/openspec-apply-change <other>`).

2. **Check status to understand the schema**
   ```bash
   openspec status --change "<name>" --json
   ```
   Parse the JSON to understand:
   - `schemaName`: The workflow being used (e.g., "spec-driven")
   - `planningHome`, `changeRoot`, and `actionContext`: planning scope and edit constraints
   - Which artifact contains the tasks (typically "tasks" for spec-driven, check status for others)

3. **Get apply instructions**

   ```bash
   openspec instructions apply --change "<name>" --json
   ```

   This returns:
   - `contextFiles`: artifact ID -> array of concrete file paths (varies by schema - could be proposal/specs/design/tasks or spec/tests/implementation/docs)
   - Progress (total, complete, remaining)
   - Task list with status, source path, and source line
   - Dynamic instruction based on current state
   - Optional `context`: current required project instruction input from the selected root
   - Optional `operationGuidance`: current advisory guidance for apply
   - `missingArtifacts` (when present): required artifact ids with no output

   **Handle states:**
   - If `state: "blocked"`: show the message and pause implementation.
     - If `missingArtifacts` is non-empty: suggest completing the missing artifacts. Run `openspec status --change "<name>" --json`, select the next `ready` artifact (not `skipped` or `blocked`), and use `openspec instructions "<artifact-id>" --change "<name>" --json` for its rules and template. Keep the selected `--store <id>` on both commands.
     - Otherwise, follow the CLI instruction to create or repair the schema-configured tracking file from existing planning artifacts. Do not assume another artifact is ready or start implementation while blocked.
   - If `state: "all_done"`: congratulate, suggest archive
   - Otherwise: proceed to implementation

4. **Read context files**

   Read every file path listed under `contextFiles` from the apply instructions output.
   The files depend on the schema being used:
   - **spec-driven**: proposal, specs, design, tasks
   - Other schemas: follow the contextFiles from CLI output

   Do not copy `context` or `operationGuidance` verbatim into implementation
   files or planning artifacts unless the user separately asks for that content.

5. **Show current progress**

   Display:
   - Schema being used
   - Progress: "N/M tasks complete"
   - Remaining tasks overview
   - Dynamic instruction from CLI

6. **Implement tasks (loop until done or blocked)**

   For each pending task:
   - Show which task is being worked on
   - Make the code changes required
   - Keep changes minimal and focused
   - Before editing, confirm the checkbox at the returned `sourcePath` and `line` still matches the task description; if it does not, rerun the apply instructions and use the refreshed location
   - Mark the task complete at its returned `sourcePath` and `line`: `- [ ]` → `- [x]`
   - Rerun the apply instructions and confirm that task is now done and progress changed
   - Continue to next task

   **Pause if:**
   - Task is unclear → ask for clarification
   - Implementation reveals a design issue → suggest updating artifacts
   - A task needs work beyond what the spec and tasks describe, or you are tempted to drop, narrow, defer, or accept exceptions to specified behavior to make it fit → surface the added scope and ask; do not absorb it silently
   - Error or blocker encountered → report and wait for guidance
   - User interrupts

7. **On completion or pause, show status**

   Display:
   - Tasks completed this session
   - Overall progress: "N/M tasks complete"
   - If all done: suggest archive
   - If paused: explain why and wait for guidance

**Output During Implementation**

```
## Implementing: <change-name> (schema: <schema-name>)

Working on task 3/7: <task description>
[...implementation happening...]
✓ Task complete

Working on task 4/7: <task description>
[...implementation happening...]
✓ Task complete
```

**Output On Completion**

```
## Implementation Complete

**Change:** <change-name>
**Schema:** <schema-name>
**Progress:** 7/7 tasks complete ✓

### Completed This Session
- [x] Task 1
- [x] Task 2
...

All tasks complete! You can archive this change with `/openspec-archive-change`.
```

**Output On Pause (Issue Encountered)**

```
## Implementation Paused

**Change:** <change-name>
**Schema:** <schema-name>
**Progress:** 4/7 tasks complete

### Issue Encountered
<description of the issue>

**Options:**
1. <option 1>
2. <option 2>
3. Other approach

What would you like to do?
```

**Guardrails**
- Keep going through tasks until done or blocked
- Always read context files before starting (from the apply instructions output)
- If task is ambiguous, pause and ask before implementing
- If implementation reveals issues, suggest updating artifacts
- Keep code changes minimal and focused
- Update task checkbox immediately after completing each task
- Pause on errors, blockers, or unclear requirements - don't guess
- When a task needs work beyond what the spec describes, surface the added scope and pause - never silently narrow, defer, or simplify away specified behavior
- Only mark a task `- [x]` when its specified behavior is fully implemented, not when it is partially done or deferred
- Use contextFiles from CLI output, don't assume specific file names
- Use each task's sourcePath and line to update its exact checkbox
- Do not use context or operation guidance as proof that a task is complete
- Apply relevant project context; report conflicts with controlling workflow inputs
- Consider every guidance entry; explain any inapplicable or conflicting advice
- Do not copy runtime context or operation guidance into implementation files or planning artifacts
- Preserve CLI-controlled blocked/ready/all-done behavior and completion criteria

**Fluid Workflow Integration**

This skill supports the "actions on a change" model:

- **Can be invoked anytime**: Before all artifacts are done (if tasks exist), after partial implementation, interleaved with other actions
- **Allows artifact updates**: If implementation reveals design issues, suggest updating artifacts - not phase-locked, work fluidly

# Skill: openspec-apply-change
> Implement tasks from an OpenSpec change. Use when the user wants to start implementing, continue implementation, or work through tasks. Also use when the user says "openspec apply", "opsx apply", or "openspec implement".
(scope: project · /home/kenzanin/tmp/goIsekai/.agents/skills/openspec-apply-change/SKILL.md)

Implement tasks from an OpenSpec change.

**Store selection:** If the user names a store (a store is a standalone OpenSpec repo registered on this machine) or the work lives in one, run `openspec store list --json` to discover registered store ids, then pass `--store <id>` on the commands that read or write specs and changes (`new change`, `status`, `instructions`, `list`, `show`, `validate`, `archive`, `doctor`, `context`, `schemas`, `view`). Once selected, treat `--store <id>` as sticky for the rest of the workflow. Every unscoped example of those commands below is shorthand: before running it, append the flag. For example, run `openspec status --change "<name>" --json --store "<id>"`, not the unscoped form shown below. Other commands do not take the flag. Hints printed by commands already carry the flag; keep it on follow-ups. Without a store, commands act on the nearest local `openspec/` root.

**Project check:** These steps expect a project that already uses OpenSpec. Before the first step that writes anything (`new change`, `archive`, `sync specs`, or authoring an artifact file), confirm the project has a root: run `openspec list --json` (with `--store <id>` when a store is selected, since the store is then the root) and read `root`. A root object means the project is set up. `"root": null` means it is not - there is no `openspec/` directory here, and a write such as `openspec new change` would create one as a side effect. The command also exits non-zero, which is that answer rather than a broken CLI, so read the JSON instead of retrying or working around it.

One `"root": null` is not about setup: when a `status` error message starts with `Declared in` or `Invalid store declaration in` and names this project's `openspec/config.yaml` (or `config.yml`), the project does use OpenSpec through a store it declares, which this machine cannot resolve (the store is not registered, or the `store:` line is malformed). Do not treat it as uninitialized and skip the branches below: stop before writing and show the user that error's `message` and `fix`.

Otherwise, with no root, what happens next depends on how this workflow was reached:

- **Auto-selected**: you chose this workflow yourself, without the user naming OpenSpec, naming this skill, or running its slash command. Stop using OpenSpec and answer the request normally, as you would with no OpenSpec installed. Do not ask them to set anything up and do not mention OpenSpec setup.
- **Explicit OpenSpec request**: the user named OpenSpec, named this skill, or ran its slash command. Stop before writing and ask how to proceed: set this project up (`openspec init`), target a store they already have (`--store <id>`), or continue without OpenSpec for this request. Wait for their answer.

In both branches, never create the root as a side effect: do not run `openspec init` until the user asks for it, do not hand-create `openspec/` files, and do not let a command create it.

`<capability-path>` is the spec directory relative to `specs/` (for example, `user-auth` or `identity/user-auth`). Preserve the full path from each delta spec when resolving its main spec.

**Input**: Optionally specify a change name (e.g., `/openspec-apply-change add-auth`). If omitted, check if it can be inferred from conversation context. If vague or ambiguous you MUST prompt for available changes.

**Steps**

1. **Select the change**

   If a name is provided, use it. Otherwise:
   - Infer from conversation context if the user mentioned a change
   - Auto-select if only one active change exists
   - If ambiguous, run `openspec list --json` to get available changes and ask the user to select one

   Always announce: "Using change: <name>" and how to override (e.g., `/openspec-apply-change <other>`).

2. **Check status to understand the schema**
   ```bash
   openspec status --change "<name>" --json
   ```
   Parse the JSON to understand:
   - `schemaName`: The workflow being used (e.g., "spec-driven")
   - `planningHome`, `changeRoot`, and `actionContext`: planning scope and edit constraints
   - Which artifact contains the tasks (typically "tasks" for spec-driven, check status for others)

3. **Get apply instructions**

   ```bash
   openspec instructions apply --change "<name>" --json
   ```

   This returns:
   - `contextFiles`: artifact ID -> array of concrete file paths (varies by schema - could be proposal/specs/design/tasks or spec/tests/implementation/docs)
   - Progress (total, complete, remaining)
   - Task list with status, source path, and source line
   - Dynamic instruction based on current state
   - Optional `context`: current required project instruction input from the selected root
   - Optional `operationGuidance`: current advisory guidance for apply
   - `missingArtifacts` (when present): required artifact ids with no output

   **Handle states:**
   - If `state: "blocked"`: show the message and pause implementation.
     - If `missingArtifacts` is non-empty: suggest completing the missing artifacts. Run `openspec status --change "<name>" --json`, select the next `ready` artifact (not `skipped` or `blocked`), and use `openspec instructions "<artifact-id>" --change "<name>" --json` for its rules and template. Keep the selected `--store <id>` on both commands.
     - Otherwise, follow the CLI instruction to create or repair the schema-configured tracking file from existing planning artifacts. Do not assume another artifact is ready or start implementation while blocked.
   - If `state: "all_done"`: congratulate, suggest archive
   - Otherwise: proceed to implementation

4. **Read context files**

   Read every file path listed under `contextFiles` from the apply instructions output.
   The files depend on the schema being used:
   - **spec-driven**: proposal, specs, design, tasks
   - Other schemas: follow the contextFiles from CLI output

   Do not copy `context` or `operationGuidance` verbatim into implementation
   files or planning artifacts unless the user separately asks for that content.

5. **Show current progress**

   Display:
   - Schema being used
   - Progress: "N/M tasks complete"
   - Remaining tasks overview
   - Dynamic instruction from CLI

6. **Implement tasks (loop until done or blocked)**

   For each pending task:
   - Show which task is being worked on
   - Make the code changes required
   - Keep changes minimal and focused
   - Before editing, confirm the checkbox at the returned `sourcePath` and `line` still matches the task description; if it does not, rerun the apply instructions and use the refreshed location
   - Mark the task complete at its returned `sourcePath` and `line`: `- [ ]` → `- [x]`
   - Rerun the apply instructions and confirm that task is now done and progress changed
   - Continue to next task

   **Pause if:**
   - Task is unclear → ask for clarification
   - Implementation reveals a design issue → suggest updating artifacts
   - A task needs work beyond what the spec and tasks describe, or you are tempted to drop, narrow, defer, or accept exceptions to specified behavior to make it fit → surface the added scope and ask; do not absorb it silently
   - Error or blocker encountered → report and wait for guidance
   - User interrupts

7. **On completion or pause, show status**

   Display:
   - Tasks completed this session
   - Overall progress: "N/M tasks complete"
   - If all done: suggest archive
   - If paused: explain why and wait for guidance

**Output During Implementation**

```
## Implementing: <change-name> (schema: <schema-name>)

Working on task 3/7: <task description>
[...implementation happening...]
✓ Task complete

Working on task 4/7: <task description>
[...implementation happening...]
✓ Task complete
```

**Output On Completion**

```
## Implementation Complete

**Change:** <change-name>
**Schema:** <schema-name>
**Progress:** 7/7 tasks complete ✓

### Completed This Session
- [x] Task 1
- [x] Task 2
...

All tasks complete! You can archive this change with `/openspec-archive-change`.
```

**Output On Pause (Issue Encountered)**

```
## Implementation Paused

**Change:** <change-name>
**Schema:** <schema-name>
**Progress:** 4/7 tasks complete

### Issue Encountered
<description of the issue>

**Options:**
1. <option 1>
2. <option 2>
3. Other approach

What would you like to do?
```

**Guardrails**
- Keep going through tasks until done or blocked
- Always read context files before starting (from the apply instructions output)
- If task is ambiguous, pause and ask before implementing
- If implementation reveals issues, suggest updating artifacts
- Keep code changes minimal and focused
- Update task checkbox immediately after completing each task
- Pause on errors, blockers, or unclear requirements - don't guess
- When a task needs work beyond what the spec describes, surface the added scope and pause - never silently narrow, defer, or simplify away specified behavior
- Only mark a task `- [x]` when its specified behavior is fully implemented, not when it is partially done or deferred
- Use contextFiles from CLI output, don't assume specific file names
- Use each task's sourcePath and line to update its exact checkbox
- Do not use context or operation guidance as proof that a task is complete
- Apply relevant project context; report conflicts with controlling workflow inputs
- Consider every guidance entry; explain any inapplicable or conflicting advice
- Do not copy runtime context or operation guidance into implementation files or planning artifacts
- Preserve CLI-controlled blocked/ready/all-done behavior and completion criteria

**Fluid Workflow Integration**

This skill supports the "actions on a change" model:

- **Can be invoked anytime**: Before all artifacts are done (if tasks exist), after partial implementation, interleaved with other actions
- **Allows artifact updates**: If implementation reveals design issues, suggest updating artifacts - not phase-locked, work fluidly
</skill-pin>

For example, run `openspec status --change "<name>" --json --store "<id>"`, not the unscoped form shown below. Other commands do not take the flag. Hints printed by commands already carry the flag; keep it on follow-ups. Without a store, commands act on the nearest local `openspec/` root.

**Project check:** These steps expect a project that already uses OpenSpec. Before the first step that writes anything (`new change`, `archive`, `sync specs`, or authoring an artifact file), confirm the project has a root: run `openspec list --json` (with `--store <id>` when a store is selected, since the store is then the root) and read `root`. A root object means the project is set up. `"root": null` means it is not - there is no `openspec/` directory here, and a write such as `openspec new change` would create one as a side effect. The command also exits non-zero, which is that answer rather than a broken CLI, so read the JSON instead of retrying or working around it.

One `"root": null` is not about setup: when a `status` error message starts with `Declared in` or `Invalid store declaration in` and names this project's `openspec/config.yaml` (or `config.yml`), the project does use OpenSpec through a store it declares, which this machine cannot resolve (the store is not registered, or the `store:` line is malformed). Do not treat it as uninitialized and skip the branches below: stop before writing and show the user that error's `message` and `fix`.

Otherwise, with no root, what happens next depends on how this workflow was reached:

- **Auto-selected**: you chose this workflow yourself, without the user naming OpenSpec, naming this skill, or running its slash command. Stop using OpenSpec and answer the request normally, as you would with no OpenSpec installed. Do not ask them to set anything up and do not mention OpenSpec setup.
- **Explicit OpenSpec request**: the user named OpenSpec, named this skill, or ran its slash command. Stop before writing and ask how to proceed: set this project up (`openspec init`), target a store they already have (`--store <id>`), or continue without OpenSpec for this request. Wait for their answer.

In both branches, never create the root as a side effect: do not run `openspec init` until the user asks for it, do not hand-create `openspec/` files, and do not let a command create it.

`<capability-path>` is the spec directory relative to `specs/` (for example, `user-auth` or `identity/user-auth`). Preserve the full path from each delta spec when resolving its main spec.

**Input**: Optionally specify a change name (e.g., `/openspec-apply-change add-auth`). If omitted, check if it can be inferred from conversation context. If vague or ambiguous you MUST prompt for available changes.

**Steps**

1. **Select the change**

   If a name is provided, use it. Otherwise:
   - Infer from conversation context if the user mentioned a change
   - Auto-select if only one active change exists
   - If ambiguous, run `openspec list --json` to get available changes and ask the user to select one

   Always announce: "Using change: <name>" and how to override (e.g., `/openspec-archive-change <other>`).

2. **Check status to understand the schema**
   ```bash
   openspec status --change "<name>" --json
   ```
   Parse the JSON to understand:
   - `schemaName`: The workflow being used (e.g., "spec-driven")
   - `planningHome`, `changeRoot`, and `actionContext`: planning scope and edit constraints
   - Which artifact contains the tasks (typically "tasks" for spec-driven, check status for others)

3. **Get apply instructions**

   ```bash
   openspec instructions apply --change "<name>" --json
   ```

   This returns:
   - `contextFiles`: artifact ID -> array of concrete file paths (varies by schema - could be proposal/specs/design/tasks or spec/tests/implementation/docs)
   - Progress (total, complete, remaining)
   - Task list with status, source path, and source line
   - Dynamic instruction based on current state
   - Optional `context`: current required project instruction input from the selected root
   - Optional `operationGuidance`: current advisory guidance for apply
   - `missingArtifacts` (when present): required artifact ids with no output

   **Handle states:**
   - If `state: "blocked"`: show the message and pause implementation.
     - If `missingArtifacts` is non-empty: suggest completing the missing artifacts. Run `openspec status --change "<name>" --json`, select the next `ready` artifact (not `skipped` or `blocked`), and use `openspec instructions "<artifact-id>" --change "<name>" --json` for its rules and template. Keep the selected `--store <id>` on both commands.
     - Otherwise, follow the CLI instruction to create or repair the schema-configured tracking file from existing planning artifacts. Do not assume another artifact is ready or start implementation while blocked.
   - If `state: "all_done"`: congratulate, suggest archive
   - Otherwise: proceed to implementation

4. **Read context files**

   Read every file path listed under `contextFiles` from the apply instructions output.
   The files depend on the schema being used:
   - **spec-driven**: proposal, specs, design, tasks
   - Other schemas: follow the contextFiles from CLI output

   Do not copy `context` or `operationGuidance` verbatim into implementation
   files or planning artifacts unless the user separately asks for that content.

5. **Show current progress**

   Display:
   - Schema being used
   - Progress: "N/M tasks complete"
   - Remaining tasks overview
   - Dynamic instruction from CLI

6. **Implement tasks (loop until done or blocked)**

   For each pending task:
   - Show which task is being worked on
   - Make the code changes required
   - Keep changes minimal and focused
   - Before editing, confirm the checkbox at the returned `sourcePath` and `line` still matches the task description; if it does not, rerun the apply instructions and use the refreshed location
   - Mark the task complete at its returned `sourcePath` and `line`: `- [ ]` → `- [x]`
   - Rerun the apply instructions and confirm that task is now done and progress changed
   - Continue to next task

   **Pause if:**
   - Task is unclear → ask for clarification
   - Implementation reveals a design issue → suggest updating artifacts
   - A task needs work beyond what the spec and tasks describe, or you are tempted to drop, narrow, defer, or accept exceptions to specified behavior to make it fit → surface the added scope and ask; do not absorb it silently
   - Error or blocker encountered → report and wait for guidance
   - User interrupts

7. **On completion or pause, show status**

   Display:
   - Tasks completed this session
   - Overall progress: "N/M tasks complete"
   - If all done: suggest archive
   - If paused: explain why and wait for guidance

**Output During Implementation**

```
## Implementing: <change-name> (schema: <schema-name>)

Working on task 3/7: <task description>
[...implementation happening...]
✓ Task complete

Working on task 4/7: <task description>
[...implementation happening...]
✓ Task complete
```

**Output On Completion**

```
## Implementation Complete

**Change:** <change-name>
**Schema:** <schema-name>
**Progress:** 7/7 tasks complete ✓

### Completed This Session
- [x] Task 1
- [x] Task 2
...

All tasks complete! You can archive this change with `/openspec-archive-change`.
```

**Output On Pause (Issue Encountered)**

```
## Implementation Paused

**Change:** <change-name>
**Schema:** <schema-name>
**Progress:** 4/7 tasks complete

### Issue Encountered
<description of the issue>

**Options:**
1. <option 1>
2. <option 2>
3. Other approach

What would you like to do?
```

**Guardrails**
- Keep going through tasks until done or blocked
- Always read context files before starting (from the apply instructions output)
- If task is ambiguous, pause and ask before implementing
- If implementation reveals issues, suggest updating artifacts
- Keep code changes minimal and focused
- Update task checkbox immediately after completing each task
- Pause on errors, blockers, or unclear requirements - don't guess
- When a task needs work beyond what the spec describes, surface the added scope and pause - never silently narrow, defer, or simplify away specified behavior
- Only mark a task `- [x]` when its specified behavior is fully implemented, not when it is partially done or deferred
- Use contextFiles from CLI output, don't assume specific file names
- Use each task's sourcePath and line to update its exact checkbox
- Do not use context or operation guidance as proof that a task is complete
- Apply relevant project context; report conflicts with controlling workflow inputs
- Consider every guidance entry; explain any inapplicable or conflicting advice
- Do not copy runtime context or operation guidance into implementation files or planning artifacts
- Preserve CLI-controlled blocked/ready/all-done behavior and completion criteria

**Fluid Workflow Integration**

This skill supports the "actions on a change" model:

- **Can be invoked anytime**: Before all artifacts are done (if tasks exist), after partial implementation, interleaved with other actions
- **Allows artifact updates**: If implementation reveals design issues, suggest updating artifacts - not phase-locked, work fluidly
</skill-pin>

<execution-policy preset="balanced" version="3">
verify=targeted
</execution-policy>

## User

nah diskusi sekarang bro. selain genre dan status apalagi bro yang bisa di sederhanakan seperti ini? apakah format tanggal?

## Assistant

Sebelum jawab, aku cek dulu apa yang benar-benar ada di kode — bukan menduga.

Ada `internal/pluginutil/dates.go` — itu kandidat kuat. Baca isinya + cari pemakainya.

Data harinya sudah kekumpul. Cek tiga kandidat lain sebelum menyimpulkan.

Ada concept OKF yang persis soal ini. Ambil + cek satu kandidat lagi.

Satu grep terakhir biar daftar native-nya akurat.

Ya — **tanggal**, dan itu satu-satunya yang benar-benar setara. Ini alasannya.

## Kenapa tanggal setara dengan status

`pluginutil/dates.go` (129 baris) melakukan persis yang dulu dilakukan `DefaultStatusMap`: **host menebak ejaan situs**.

| Isi | Detail |
|---|---|
| `dateLayouts` | 15 layout dicoba berurutan: RFC3339, `2006-01-02 15:04`, `02 Jan 2006`, `Jan 2, 2006`, RFC1123, RFC822, … |
| `relativeUnits` | `minute/min/hour/hr/day/week/month/year` → durasi |
| `parseRelative` | `"just now"`, `"today"`, `"moments ago"`, `"yesterday"`, regex `N unit ago` |
| `isDigits` | panjang digit ditebak: 9–11 = unix detik, 12–14 = milidetik |
| Native | `text.date_to_iso` |

Dan kode itu **mengakui sendiri tidak bisa**: *"Slash-only dates never resolve: day-first and month-first are indistinguishable without the site's locale."* Itu kalimat yang sama bentuknya dengan "plugin tidak sepakat soal ejaan" di kasus status — host menyimpulkan dia tidak punya informasi yang cukup, lalu bail.

## Kalau diubah, yang hilang

Validator saja: terima RFC 3339 → keluarkan RFC 3339; selain itu `""`. Yang dihapus: `dateLayouts`, `relativeUnits`, `parseRelative`, `relativeDate`, `isDigits`, `ParseDate`, `DateToISO`, `formatISO` — **~110 dari 129 baris**.

Kontraknya sudah cocok: komentar di file itu bilang `""` harus membuat caller **omit** `released_at`, karena ABI men-decode ke `time.Time` dan menolak `""`. Jadi validator masuk tanpa mengubah ABI.

## Biaya jujur

- **23 dari 41 plugin live** mem-parse tanggal, dan semuanya sekarang menyerahkan string mentah situs ke host (pola: `find_one` + `text.date_to_iso` di Go/TinyGo, regex + `date_to_iso` di Lua, `normalize_madara_date` di Lua Madara, `abi.date_to_iso` di Odin). Kalau host berhenti menebak, mereka harus punya layout sendiri — tapi tiap situs kan cuma **satu** format, jadi itu justru lebih sedikit menebak, bukan lebih banyak.
- Tanggal relatif (`"3 days ago"`) tidak bisa jadi validator — plugin yang hitung sendiri pakai clock-nya. Plugin lebih tahu.
- Gagalnya senyap: plugin yang belum migrasi kehilangan tanggal chapter (bukan crash). Ini yang bikin harus bertahap, sama seperti status.

Catatan: setelah status jadi validator dan genre jadi kanonik, `date_to_iso` adalah **native `text.*` terakhir yang masih menebak**. Jadi ini langkah penutup yang wajar.

## Kandidat lain

| Kandidat | Verdict |
|---|---|
| `text.chapter_num` | Bau yang sama — regex marker, guard `Vol. 3`, fallback angka pertama, dan komentarnya mengaku `"chapter-12-5"` ambigu. **Tapi** 168 pemakaian di 41 file. Kandidat nyata, blast radius jauh lebih besar. Bukan sekarang. |
| Language | Host **tidak** menangani sama sekali (grep kosong). Tidak ada yang perlu disederhanakan; menambah vocabulary justru YAGNI. |
| Demographic / Manhwa | Sudah verbatim, tidak ada kamus. Tidak ada pekerjaan. |
| Section config | Tinggal `[enhance]` (ID plugin → mode), plus `[workers]`/`[tray]`/image format. Itu knob host sendiri, bukan terjemahan ejaan situs. Aman. |
| `normalize_title` | Fungsi kunci (lowercase/strip), satu definisi, internal host. Bukan vocabulary drift. |

## Rekomendasi

Urutannya: **audit dulu, jangan potong dulu.** Kamu sudah punya konsep OKF `0150-live-plugins-date-info-status` yang memetakan status tanggal 41 plugin — itu titik mulai yang tepat, bukan grep ulang. Setelah tahu siapa yang benar-benar bergantung pada tebakan host, baru bikin change `host-date-source-of-truth` dengan pola yang sama: host validator, plugin adapt, yang tak dikenal = omit (bukan `""` yang bocor ke DB).

Mau aku mulai dari audit 41 plugin itu (hasilnya jadi lampiran proposal), atau langsung tulis proposal openspec-nya dengan asumsi audit menyusul?

## Tiga hal yang perlu diputuskan

**1. `time.Time` tidak bisa menampung `"recently"`.** Kalau plugin mengirim angka epoch ke field `time.Time`, decode JSON-nya **gagal** — dan itu bukan cuma tanggalnya hilang, tapi **seluruh chapter list gagal** (komentar di `dates.go` sudah memperingatkan ini soal `""`). Jadi "yang tak bisa dikonversi tetap string" menuntut field kedua, bukan sekadar nilai berbeda di field yang sama.

**2. Tidak ada satu pun UI yang menampilkan `released_at`.** Aku grep seluruh `*.lua`/`*.js`/`*.qml`/`*.go` — nol hit di luar lapisan DB. Jadi kalau kita simpan `"recently"` sebagai teks, hari ini **tidak ada yang membacanya**: data write-only. Kolom teks baru hanya berguna kalau chapter list benar-benar menampilkannya.

**3. `"four weeks ago"` sebenarnya bisa dikonversi — dan kalau disimpan sebagai teks, dia membusuk.** Disimpan sebagai teks, tahun depan masih tertulis "four weeks ago" padahal itu chapter lama. Dikonversi sekali saat fetch, jadi tanggal tetap yang perkiraan tapi stabil. Ini beda kelas dengan `"recently"` yang memang tidak membawa informasi waktu.

## Yang tetap aku setujui

Epoch lebih baik daripada RFC3339 di sisi plugin: situs yang sudah memberi angka (atsumaru: epoch millis) tak perlu format apa pun, dan yang scraping pakai `os.time{}` / `Date.UTC(...)` — mereka tahu locale situsnya, host tidak (kode host sendiri mengaku tak bisa membedakan day-first vs month-first).

Bagian itu aku keliru — kalau tanggalnya benar-benar dirender, itu mengubah desainnya. Aku cek jalur tampilnya.

Sekarang aku buat change openspec host-side-nya. Cek dulu capability mana yang tersentuh.

## Isi `host-date-source-of-truth` (host-only)

| Artefak | Isi |
|---|---|
| `proposal.md` | Kenapa host berhenti menebak; apa yang berubah; non-goals eksplisit |
| `specs/plugin-abi/spec.md` | 2 ADDED requirement: *wire shape* (epoch detik, atau RFC 3339 selama migrasi; host route by tipe JSON) dan *unusable date retained, not fatal* (prosa disimpan verbatim, angka di luar 1970–2100 jadi "tanpa tanggal" + warning) |
| `specs/storage/spec.md` | MODIFIED `Chapter persistence` + kolom `released_text`; `released_at` tetap TIMESTAMP dan NULL-means-unknown, jadi tidak ada row yang ditulis ulang |
| `design.md` | 7 keputusan (D1–D7) + risiko + migration plan |
| `tasks.md` | 4 seksi, 13 task, semuanya host-side |

Keputusan yang terkunci sesuai jawabanmu:
- **Epoch detik, satu unit.** Situs yang kasih milidetik membagi 1000 di plugin — mendeteksi unit dari jumlah digit itu justru bentuk menebak yang mau dihapus.
- **Satu field input.** `released_at` menerima number (epoch) atau string; string yang bukan RFC 3339 jadi `ReleasedText` (internal, `json:"-"`). Plugin tidak perlu belajar key baru.
- **`time.Time` tetap.** Routing ditaruh di `UnmarshalJSON` `types.Chapter`, jadi ~40 paket hilir tidak berubah bentuk.
- **Guard 1970–2100**, bukan heuristik satuan: tanpa ini epoch-millis tampil sebagai tahun 58600.
- **Render pakai slot yang sudah ada.** `detail_chapter_row.lua` sudah punya cabang `elseif type(c.ReleasedAt) == "string"`, jadi prosa tidak butuh cabang baru.

## Satu hal yang perlu kamu sadari

`text.date_to_iso` **tidak** dihapus di change ini — hanya ditandai deprecated. Alasannya: 23 plugin live masih memanggilnya, dan kalau tebakan layout-nya dimatikan sekarang, **semua tanggal chapter hilang** sampai change plugin selesai. Jadi pembersihan `dateLayouts`/`relativeUnits`/`parseRelative` itu jadi deliverable change berikutnya, bareng migrasi plugin ke epoch.

Kalau kamu lebih suka tebakan itu dimatikan sekarang dan menerima tanggal kosong sementara, bilang — scope-nya tinggal geser satu seksi.

Konsekuensi lain: `ReleasedText` belum akan terisi sampai plugin mengirim prosa mentah (sekarang mereka memanggil `date_to_iso` yang mengembalikan `""` untuk "recently"). Kolomnya siap, tapi jalur teks baru hidup setelah change plugin.

## Temuan dari manual (dan binary)

`max_steps` **tidak bisa** di-set permanen di level worker:

| Jalur | Status |
|---|---|
| `[agent].max_steps` di config.toml | **mati** — binary: *"`[agent].max_steps and planner_max_steps` are no longer used; use the CLI `--max-steps` flag"* |
| Frontmatter `SKILL.md` | **tidak dikenal** — yang terbukti hanya `runAs`, `model`, `allowed-tools` |
| `reasonix doctor capabilities --json` | entri worker cuma memuat `name/description/scope/path/status/run_as` — tidak ada field step |
| Argumen `task({max_steps: N})` | **inilah yang bekerja** |

Baris `max_steps: 120` sempat kutulis di frontmatter worker, lalu **kurombak lagi** — kunci yang diabaikan diam-diam lebih berbahaya daripada tidak ada, karena kelihatan seolah sudah di-set.

## Yang sekarang berlaku

**`REASONIX.md`** (baru, root repo — nama native Reasonix, dimuat ke system prompt, tidak menabrak blok rtk di `CLAUDE.md` atau isi OKF di `AGENTS.md`):

> **Pecah setiap delegasi menjadi task sekecil mungkin** — satu task = satu paket, satu kelompok file, atau satu artefak OpenSpec.
> - Change >~4 task: delegasikan bertahap, lanjutkan transkrip yang sama via `task(task_id: "sa_...")`.
> - **Selalu set `max_steps: 120` eksplisit** di setiap `task({profile: "worker", max_steps: 120, ...})`.
> - Verifikasi hasilnya — model murah melaporkan sukses pada kode yang tidak compile (worker pernah menyerahkan test error sintaks + nol checkbox).

## Dua cara membuat 120 jadi default

1. **Per panggilan** (yang sekarang didokumentasikan) — aman, eksplisit, tapi aku harus ingat tiap kali.
2. **Naikkan cap induk** — subagent default = separuh cap induk, jadi `reasonix --max-steps 240` bikin default worker otomatis 120 tanpa perlu diingat. Ini satu-satunya cara bikin 120 jadi bawaan, tapi berlaku saat launch, bukan sesuatu yang bisa kutulis ke file.

## 1. Pagination — aku belum tahu gejalanya

Yang **pasti** salah, hasil baca kode:

`internal/httpserver/views_library.go:136-144` — `page` cuma dijepit di batas bawah:

```go
page, _ := strconv.Atoi(r.URL.Query().Get("page"))
if page < 1 { page = 1 }        // tidak ada clamp atas
total := len(mangas)
start := min((page-1)*pageSize, total)
end   := min(start+pageSize, total)
```

`/view/library?page=999` → `start=end=total` → **grid kosong tanpa pesan apa pun**, padahal `TotalPages` benar. Tidak ada redirect ke halaman terakhir.

Yang **sudah benar** (biar tidak salah tuduh): `pagBase = libraryLink({})` menyusun ulang seluruh query dari filter aktif tanpa key `page`, lalu `pageURL()` menambah `&page=N`. Jadi filter tidak hilang saat pindah halaman, dan tidak ada `page` ganda. Mekanisme ini sehat.

Sisanya tergantung gejala yang kamu lihat — bisa jadi bukan clamp itu.

## 2. quickjs — asumsiku salah, dan itu mengubah kesimpulan

Aku sempat mengira cznic/quickjs butuh cgo. **Tidak.** Sudah kuverifikasi dari repo-nya (clone ke `$TMPDIR`):

- Deskripsi resmi: *"a pure-Go, CGo-free embeddable JavaScript engine (ES2023)"*, ditranspilasi dari C pakai `ccgo`. Nol `import "C"` di 42 file `.go`.
- Deps: `modernc.org/libquickjs` + `modernc.org/libc`. Dan `modernc.org/libc` **sudah ada di `go.sum` kita** (ditarik `modernc.org/sqlite`), jadi beban dependency-nya lebih ringan dari dugaan.
- Ada `SetEvalTimeout` / `Interrupt` → sandboxing tetap bisa.
- Ada folder `compare/` berisi benchmark lawan goja.

Jadi secara prinsip **bisa**. Tapi biayanya bukan di engine-nya:

| | goja sekarang | quickjs |
|---|---|---|
| Permukaan kode | 14 file, **1.586 baris** (`js*.go`) | harus ditulis ulang semua |
| Interop Go | idiomatik (`ToValue`, `ExportTo`, `AssertFunction`) | nilai JS berupa `*quickjs.Value`, konversi manual |
| `Intl` | ada | tidak ada (kecuali opt-in `quickjs/intl`) |

Yang berat bukan mesinnya, tapi `js_natives.go`, `js_crypto.go`, `js_html.go`, `js_http.go`, `js_regex.go` — puluhan fungsi host yang didaftarkan ke runtime. Semua itu ikut dikonversi. Dan motivasi performa lemah di sini: plugin kita **I/O-bound** (HTTP fetch + parse HTML), bukan loop CPU.

Rekomendasiku: **jangan swap**, kecuali ada blocker spesifik di goja.

## 3. Bahasa lain untuk plugin, CGO=0

Kamu **sudah punya** jawabannya, dan itu lengkap. Inventaris `app_data/plugins`: **24 lua, 19 wasm, 1 go (yaegi), 1 js** (+2 `.so`). Empat engine aktif: `lunar` (Lua, pure Go), `goja` (JS), `yaegi` (Go interpreter), `wazero` (WASM).

Untuk CGO=0, WASM adalah pintu universal — Rust, Zig, C, TinyGo, AssemblyScript semuanya bisa. Kamu bahkan sudah punya `./tmp/tinygo-hello`. Jadi "bahasa lain" praktisnya = **tulis di bahasa apa pun, compile ke wasm**.

Kandidat teoritis lain (Starlark, Tengo, CEL) semuanya kalah: tidak ada yang menambah kemampuan di luar Lua+WASM, dan semuanya butuh port surface baru.

**Ini insight arsitekturnya:** biaya menambah engine ke-5 bukan engine-nya, tapi **surface ABI × N**. Setiap bahasa harus punya sendiri: `lua_html/js_html/yaegi_surface/wasm_host_helpers`, `lua_crypto/js_crypto/...`, `lua_browser/js_browser/...`. Itu sebabnya `helper_parity_test.go` (14,7K) ada — untuk menjaga keempatnya tidak menyimpang. Engine kelima = porting seluruh helper a kelima kalinya.

Jadi saranku: **jangan tambah bahasa**. Kalau ada yang butuh bahasa baru, rutenya WASM.

## Soal quickjs: aku harus bilang terus terang ini kemungkinan tidak akan membuat plugin lebih cepat

Alasanmu "cari performa" — di sini justru paling lemah, dan ini bukan opini:

**Plugin kita I/O-bound, bukan CPU-bound.** Waktu habis di HTTP ke mangadex/fenyk, parse HTML, dan decode JSON — bukan di loop JS. Engine bisa 2× lebih cepat dan wall-clock per sync hampir tidak bergerak, karena engine-nya bukan yang ditunggu.

**Biaya yang nyata:**
- Surface `js*.go` = **1.586 baris / 14 file** harus ditulis ulang ke API quickjs

- `helper_parity_test.go` (14,7K) ada justru untuk menjaga 4 engine tidak menyimpang — engine ke-5 menambah satu kolom lagi ke matriks itu
- `wazero` sudah ada di go.mod, jadi JS-via-WASM (QuickJS C asli di WASM) pun sebenarnya opsi yang lebih murah daripada binding baru

Dan satu risiko yang mudah terlewat: **19 dari 27 plugin kamu adalah WASM**, bukan JS. Plugin JS cuma 1. Jadi optimasi engine JS menyentuh sekitar 1 plugin, sementara WASM-jalan-di-wazero yang menyentuh 19 plugin justru tidak kamu sebut.

**Saranku:** ukur dulu sebelum bayar 1.586 baris. Aku bisa bikin spike di `$TMPDIR` (bukan repo): satu plugin JS jalan di goja vs quickjs, `hyperfine`/`go test -bench`, laporkan angka. ## Soal quickjs — alasanmu mengubah jawabanku

Kalau repo ini memang **harness uji coba library Go**, maka "performa" bukan kriteria yang tepat, dan itu tidak masalah. Yang penting bukan engine mana yang lebih cepat, tapi **seberapa murah mengganti engine**. Dari fakta yang sudah kucek:

- `internal/pluginmanager/` punya **4 engine** (lua 2.538 baris, js 1.586 baris, yaegi, wasm), masing-masing dengan surface helper sendiri: `*_html`, `*_crypto`, `*_browser`, `*_natives`.
- `helper_parity_test.go` (14,7K) ada justru untuk menjaga keempatnya konsisten.
- Jadi menambah quickjs = kolom kelima di matriks itu.

Konsekuensinya: kalau tujuannya eksperimen, swap-nya **layak** — asal dikerjakan sebagai *spike di branch*, bukan rewrite di `main`. Kalau nanti terbukti bagus baru dipromosikan. Yang tidak worth it adalah mengerjakannya setengah jalan lalu ditinggal, karena surface yang menggantung jadi beban `helper_parity_test`.

Dan `modernc.org/libc` sudah ada di `go.sum` (dari `modernc.org/sqlite`), jadi biaya dependensinya kecil — bukan alasan untuk menolak.

## Verdict: feasible, biayanya terbatas

| Fakta | Angka |
|---|---|
| File produksi yang import goja | 14 |
| Baris `js_*.go` (produksi) | **1.198** |
| + test (`js_test`, `js_console_test`, `js_html_test`) | 388 |
| Plugin JS yang harus tetap jalan | **1** (`app_data/plugins/mangzio/main.js`, 253 baris) |

Dan plugin itu **ES5 murni** — nol `async`/`await`/arrow/`class`/`let`/`const`/template-literal. Cuma `function` + `var`. Native yang dipakainya pun sedikit: `log.*`, `http.get_body`, `text.json_blob/strip_html/normalize_status/date_to_iso`, `html.match`, `re.exec`. Tidak menyentuh crypto/browser. Jadi risiko "plugin-nya rusak" kecil.

## quickjs memang layak (sudah kuverifikasi, bukan asumsi)

- **Pure Go, tanpa cgo** — nol `import "C"` di 42 file, ditranspilasi `ccgo`. Cocok dengan `CGO_ENABLED=0`.
- API-nya memetakan hampir 1:1: `NewVM`/`Close`/`Eval`/`Compiler`/`SetPropertyValue`/`NewObjectValue`, `RegisterFunc(name, fn any, wantThis)`.
- Timeout & sandbox: `SetEvalTimeout`, `Interrupt`, `SetMemoryLimit`, `SetMaxStackSize` — malah lebih kaya dari goja.
- `modernc.org/libc` **sudah di `go.sum`** (dari `modernc.org/sqlite`), jadi beban dependency nyaris nol.

**Bonus yang menguntungkan:** `RegisterFunc` menerima `func(...any) any` — Go function langsung, tanpa `ToValue`/`Export` manual. Padahal sekarang ada **40× `.ToValue()` + 28× `.Export()`**. Sebagian boilerplate itu bisa hilang, bukan cuma dipindah.

## Tiga risiko nyata (bukan teori)

1. **`Keys()` tidak ada padanannya.** goja punya `obj.Keys()`, dipakai di `js_crypto.go:22` dan `js_natives.go:140-141`. Di quickjs harus lewat `Object.keys()` via eval atau `MarshalJSON`. Ini satu-satunya gap API yang kutemukan.
2. **Konversi mengembalikan `(T, error)`**, bukan `Value` langsung. Setiap call site berubah bentuk — lebih verbose, tapi error-nya jadi eksplisit (bagus untuk plugin, jelek untuk diff).
3. **goja bocor keluar "pulau" `js_*`** di 2 tempat: `manager.go:37` (`js *goja.Runtime`) dan `params_native.go:26-27` (`jsPluginParam(vm *goja.Runtime) func(goja.FunctionCall) goja.Value`). Dua ini wajib ikut diubah.

## Soal steer-mu: jalur plugin memang perlu disederhanakan — dan itu justru menghemat kerja swap

Ini bukti duplikasinya sekarang:

| Lokasi | Isi |
|---|---|
| `api.go:28-43` | 5× `if p.kind == "…"` berurutan untuk dispatch |
| `reload.go:27-38` + `:121` | switch teardown per-jenis, `lua` muncul 2× |
| `lifecycle.go` | 5 titik cek `kind` |
| `manager.go:25-45` | **7 field runtime** di satu struct: `lunar`, `goLib`, `goFns`, `js`, `yaegi`, `wasmMod`, `wasmFns` |
| `discovery.go` | urutan penting: `wasm` sebelum `yaegi` |

Artinya menambah/mengganti engine sekarang menyentuh **4 tempat sekaligus**. Kalau kita bikin `type engine interface` + `map[string]engine` (satu tempat registrasi), maka swap goja→quickjs **cuma menyentuh 1 file**. Refactor-nya membayar dirinya sendiri di swap ini saja — belum lagi untuk plugin yang sering copas-pasang nanti.

## Saran urutan untuk openspec-mu

Dua change terpisah, jangan digabung:

**Change 1 — `plugin-engine-registry`** (kecil, mekanis, aman)
- `engine` interface minimal: `load`, `call`, `close` — persis yang dilakukan 3 switch tree itu sekarang
- `map[kind]engine` sebagai satu-satunya titik daftar
- `manager.go` kehilangan 7 field per-jenis, diganti satu `engine` handle
- Bukti selesai: `go test ./internal/pluginmanager/` hijau + `helper_parity_test.go` tetap lulus, 24 lua + 1 js + 1 go + 19 wasm tetap jalan

**Change 2 — `plugin-js-engine-quickjs`** (setelah change 1)
- Swap di balik interface: goja → `modernc.org/quickjs`
- Wajib: `Keys()` diganti `Object.keys()`, `SetEvalTimeout`/`Interrupt` untuk deadline
- Bukti selesai: mangzio tetap bisa search + getChapterList + getPageList, `CGO_ENABLED=0 go build` lolos, `go build` tanpa `dop251/goja` sama sekali di `go list -deps`

Kalau mau lebih hemat lagi, change 2 bisa **menghapus** `js_html_find.go`/`js_browser.go` kalau memang tidak ada plugin JS yang pakai — perlu kucek dulu sebelum kamu tulis tasks-nya.

## Masalah yang kukenal

quickjs (modernc.org) **mendukung ES2023 penuh** — `async/await`, `class`, `module`, `for...of`, generator, `Map/Set` global, `JSON`, regex lookbehind, dst. Sementara itu, plugin JS yang terpasang (`mangzio/main.js`) hanya memakai **ES5 murni** (tidak ada `async/await`/`class`/`let`/`const`/arrow).

## Pertanyaan

1. **Apakah kita ingin mempertahankan dukungan ES5-only?** Ini adalah batasan yang ada sekarang — plugin harus ditulis dalam ES5. Dengan quickjs, kita bisa mengangkat batasan tersebut dan mendukung JS modern. Apakah itu bagian dari perubahan ini?

2. **Mau mulai port dari mana?** Saya lihat ada beberapa file yang harus diubah:
   - `js.go` — ganti `goja.New()` dengan `quickjs.NewVM()`, ubah `vm.Eval()` → `vm.Exec()` atau tetap `Eval()`, ubah `ResolveJSFn` / `CallJS`
   - `js_natives.go` — semua native helpers, `RegisterFunc` signature, `HostFunc` callback shape
   - `js_html*.go` — opaque handle + key enumeration
   - `js_browser.go`, `js_regex.go`, `js_console.go`
   - `manager.go` — field `js *goja.Runtime` → `js *quickjs.VM`, dan `Close()` path
   - `helper_parity_test.go` — pastikan tidak disentuh (karena ini adalah enforcement point)
   - Semua test file JS

Betul kamu — dan lebih baik: **helper-nya sudah ada, tidak perlu menambah apa pun**.

Di `internal/pluginmanager/host_surface.go:71-79` sudah ada 9 codec yang tersedia di Lua, JS, wasm, dan yaegi:

| Helper | Fungsi |
|---|---|
| `codecs.base64_encode` / `base64_decode` | base64 standar padded |
| `codecs.base64url_encode` / `base64url_decode` | base64url unpadded (JWT/VRF style) |
| `codecs.hex_encode` / `hex_decode` | hex |
| `codecs.b64_decode_hex`, `b64url_encode_hex`, `b64url_decode_hex` | campuran |

Jadi jalurnya memang harus begini: plugin kirim string ter-encode, host memproses dalam `[]byte`, host balik string ter-encode. Itu sudah benar.

## Temuan audit

**1. Yang kamu maksud itu benar, tapi alasannya bukan yang kita duga.**

Backend `.so` (purego `dlopen`) memang `kind: "go"`. Tapi `README` sudah menyebutnya **"native Go"** dan `loadGo` sendiri berkomentar `// loadGo opens a native .so plugin`. Jadi kode sudah sadar ini native; yang belum sadar cuma string kind-nya.

**2. Ada kebingungan kedua yang lebih penting.**

`examples/plugins/native/` dan `app_data/plugins/{roliascan,madarascans}/` berisi `main.so` **C/C++** (README: "Native plugins (C/C++ `n`, no cgo)"). Backend itu **sama** — `discoverGo()` glob `main.so`. Jadi satu backend menampung dua bahasa, sementara `yaegi` (Go script) justru kind sendiri. Artinya `kind:"go"` menyebut bahasa sumber, bukan runtime.

**3. Dua kandidat yang kamu sebut = tradeoff nyata, bukan selera.**

| | `native` | `pureGo` |
|---|---|---|
| Cakup | C/C++ **dan** Go `.so` | **hanya** Go `.so` — C/C++ salah label |
| Bentrok | tidak | tidak |
| Risiko | perlu `native` = gabungan | butuh kind baru buat C/C++ |

**4. Permukaan kompatibilitas: nol.** Tabel `plugins` **tidak punya kolom `kind`**. `Kind` cuma hidup di `LoadedPlugin` (runtime, di-mem) dan dipakai badge UI (`KIND_BADGE[p.Kind]`) plus 2 log + komentar. Tidak ada migration, tidak ada alias, tidak ada API contract.

**5. `goja` sudah keluar** dari `go.mod` — tersisa `modernc.org/quickjs v0.27.0` (+ `libquickjs` indirect).

## Audit (temuan, bukan asumsi)

Backend `.so` di-load via **purego `dlopen`** (`openNativeLib`). Jadi `kind: "go"` itu salah **dua kali**:

1. Menyebut **bahasa**, bukan runtime — dan `examples/plugins/native/` + `app_data/plugins/{roliascan,madarascans}` isinya **C/C++** (README: "Native plugins (C/C++ `n`, no cgo)"). Backend-nya menampung dua bahasa.
2. **Menabrak `yaegi`** — itu backend yang benar-benar menginterpretasi Go script.

`loadGo` sendiri sudah berkomentar `// loadGo opens a native .so plugin`. Kodenya sadar; cuma string kind-nya yang belum.

## Kenapa `native`, bukan `pureGo`

`pureGo` **salah label** untuk `.so` C/C++ yang sudah jalan di backend yang sama. `native` menampung keduanya dan cocok dengan README + nama direktori `examples/plugins/native/`.

## Yang membuat ini murah

`plugins` table **tidak punya kolom `kind`** — kubaca definisi tabelnya (`id, name, version, is_active, ...`), dan `sqlite3` benar-benar error `no such column: kind`. Kind cuma hidup di `LoadedPlugin`, di memori, di-derive ulang tiap load.

**Jadi: nol migration, nol alias, nol API contract.**

## Titik sentuh

| File | Yang berubah |
|---|---|
| `engine.go:25` | kunci registry |
| `goplug.go:124,159` | `pluginMetaFor` + `loadedPlugin.kind` |
| `goplug_call.go:90` | `registerFolderPlugin` |
| `load.go:91` | log hot-load |
| `manager.go:28`, `manager_meta.go:15` | komentar |
| `views_plugins.go:14,36` | komentar |
| `plugin_card.lua:10`, `plugin_cards.lua:15` | key badge — **label UI sudah `Native`**, cuma key-nya |

Plus 3 test file (`engine_test.go`, `engine_registry_test.go`, `goplug_native_test.go`) yang memang menge-spell `go`.

## Yang tidak kusentuh

`loadGo`, `goEngine`, `goFn` — itu nama fungsi internal, bukan kind. Merenamnya churn tanpa manfaat buat pembaca.

---

Artefaknya siap direview. Kalau mau langsung, bilang aja **apply** atau `/openspec-apply-change rename-go-kind-to-native`.
